The mitigation the product already provides is the drain (see the next entry), so the two entries should be read together: the no-grace reconcile is only reached when the drain has been cut short.

## W2-2 — the plan's "rolling restart kills in-flight work with no drain window" premise is false: the drain works and completed a 107.3s run, but no orchestrator default gives it time to
- **Invariant:** I5 (bounded recovery — documentation / deployment defaults)
- **Severity:** minor
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-2-agent-replacement.md`, Part B2 versus Parts A/B/C. Raw evidence: `w2-2/partB2-drain.txt`, `w2-2/partB-docker-events.txt`, `w2-2/partA-agent1.log`.
- **Observed:** With a stop grace longer than the work, the documented drain holds completely (`w2-2/partB2-drain.txt`). `edge-sideeffect` `7bb97900` was claimed by `agent1` at `17:21:37.531613`; `docker compose stop -t 200 agent1` was issued at `17:21:50.337Z`; the agent logged `"shutdown signal received; draining in-flight runs — press Ctrl-C again to force quit"` at `17:21:50.645653Z`, kept the run executing for a further **107.31 s**, and the run reached **`Succeeded` at `17:23:37.956706`**, followed by `"agent deregistered"` at `17:23:37.961899Z` and a clean container exit:

  ```
  exited exit=0 started=2026-07-29T17:12:42.670581621Z finished=2026-07-29T17:23:37.966350008Z
  mutex_holders: 0
  ```

  On the way back up the reconcile found nothing to fail — a drained agent leaves no `Running` runs (`w2-2/final-sweep.txt` lists exactly three `agent reconcile` lines across the whole scenario, one per restart-based arm and none for this one).

  What killed the runs in Parts A/B/C was therefore the *orchestrator's* stop grace, not a missing drain. `docker events` measured `docker compose restart`'s grace directly (`w2-2/partB-docker-events.txt`, epoch seconds):

  ```
  1785345889.394 unified-cd-ha-agent1-1 kill sig=15
  1785345890.407 unified-cd-ha-agent1-1 kill sig=9
  1785345890.753 unified-cd-ha-agent1-1 die  exit=137
  ```

  i.e. **SIGKILL 1.013 s after SIGTERM**, exit 137. Part A's agent log is consistent with the same ~1 s window (`w2-2/partA-agent1.log`: drain message at `17:12:41.264382Z`, no `"agent deregistered"` line at all, replacement process logging `"agent registered"` at `17:12:42.886068Z`) — that arm predates the `docker events` capture, so its grace is **inferred from the log gap, not measured**.
- **Expected:** `docs/configuration.md:173` documents `--drain-timeout duration  Max wait after SIGTERM before forced shutdown (0 = wait forever)` and `docs/high-availability.md:341` states "Agents also support graceful drain (stop claiming = cordon → finish in-progress Runs → exit)". The default is 0 — unbounded. The gap is that an *unbounded* drain is unreachable under every orchestrator default: Kubernetes' default `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` is 30 s, Docker's documented default stop timeout is 10 s, and bare `docker compose restart` gave **1.013 s** as measured above — an order of magnitude below the documented 10 s. **The cause of the ~1 s is not established** by this scenario (no `stop_grace_period` is set in `test/ha/docker-compose.ha.yaml`, and no further instrumentation was run); what is established is the measurement, and it is the number a re-runner must budget against. Whatever the cause, the conclusion is unchanged and only sharpened: every orchestrator default truncates a drain the product documents as unbounded. `grep -rn terminationGracePeriodSeconds docs/` matches exactly one line — `docs/high-availability.md:511`, `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 30   # drain grace period (>= 12s)`, in the **controller** Deployment. There is no agent Deployment guidance anywhere, and nothing tells an operator that an agent's pod grace must exceed the longest run or the drain they are relying on will be truncated into the no-grace reconcile of the previous entry.
- **Notes:** Filed as **minor** (docs / deployment-defaults gap) rather than major: the mechanism works, is documented, and is configurable; what is missing is the sentence connecting `--drain-timeout 0` to the orchestrator's own grace. Two concrete asks: document a recommended agent `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` / `stop_grace_period` in the same place `--drain-timeout` is described, and consider logging at shutdown how many runs are still in flight when the drain begins, so a truncated drain is visible in the agent's last log line. Also note the force-shutdown reconcile (`docs/high-availability.md:419-423`, `internal/agent/shutdown.go:26-33`) requires a **second** SIGINT/SIGTERM and so is unreachable from `docker stop` / `docker compose restart` / a Kubernetes pod deletion, all of which send one SIGTERM then SIGKILL; `grep -ic "forcing shutdown"` over both agents' logs returned **0** for this entire scenario (`w2-2/final-sweep.txt`). The docs are literally accurate (they say "an operator who skips the drain"), but the path is terminal-only in practice.

## W2-2 — minor: a run failed by the agent-replacement reconcile carries no operator-visible reason, unlike the queued-run reaper which appends one
- **Invariant:** I5 (bounded recovery — diagnosability)
- **Severity:** minor
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-2-agent-replacement.md`, Part B. Raw evidence: `w2-2/partB-poll.txt`.
- **Observed:** After `998f3dc3` was reconcile-failed, everything the operator can see through the API is a bare status change (`w2-2/partB-poll.txt`):

  ```
  {"id":"998f3dc3-...","jobName":"edge-longrun","status":"Failed","params":{},"createdAt":"2026-07-29T17:24:47.87702Z",
   "updatedAt":"2026-07-29T17:24:51.007957Z","triggeredBy":"env:UNIFIED_TOKEN","claimedBy":"agent1"}
  step_index 0 | tick | Failed | 17:24:48.603522 | 17:24:51.007086
  GET /api/v1/runs/998f3dc3-.../logs  ->  []
  ```

  There is no error/message field on the run, no message on the step, and no system log line. The only record of *why* is `controller2`'s `"agent reconcile: failed orphaned run (agent process replaced)"` at `17:24:51.010864Z` — a controller process log, not something attached to the run. This is a code-path property, not a timing artifact: neither `handleAgentReconcileRuns` (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:809-831`) nor `failOrphanedRun` (`internal/controller/stuckrun_reaper.go:76-90`) calls `AppendLog`.
- **Expected:** The queued-run reaper does exactly the right thing for its own victims — `internal/controller/queuedrun_reaper.go:66-72` appends `"run failed: no eligible agent available to claim it"` to the run at step index `-1` *before* failing it, with the in-code rationale "Record why on the run so it is visible in the log view". The two reapers disagree about whether a machine-initiated failure should explain itself on the run.
- **Notes:** The empty `logs` array is **not** by itself proof that the step produced nothing: `edge-longrun` echoes `tick N` once a second and only ran 2.4 s, so its output plausibly died in the agent's log buffer at SIGKILL — that is the separately-tracked LogPusher flush-loss issue, not this one. The claim here rests on the code read (no `AppendLog` on either reconcile path) with the empty log view as consistent evidence. Fix is one line, mirroring `queuedrun_reaper.go:70`.

## W2-2 — observation: I1 and I3 both held under agent replacement — parent Failed, linked child transitively Cancelled 3.2ms later, mutex released with the run's own transaction, successor acquired in 0.11s
- **Invariant:** I1 (run accounting), I3 (lock release)
- **Severity:** observation (negative control)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-2-agent-replacement.md`, Parts A and C. Raw evidence: `w2-2/partA-link-poll.txt`, `w2-2/partA-controller-reconcile.txt`, `w2-2/partC-mutex.txt`, `w2-2/final-sweep.txt`.
- **Observed:** **Part A (I1, cross-agent cascade).** The parent→child link was confirmed present *before* injection — `--max-concurrent` defaults to 1 (`docs/configuration.md:169`), so the parent occupied `agent1`'s only slot and the child was claimed by `agent2`, giving a genuine cross-agent cascade (`w2-2/partA-link-poll.txt`):

  ```
  10c7834e-2361-406b-b33e-22482abfc901|edge-call-parent|Running|agent1|17:11:04.816503
  503a3d6e-5295-4057-81aa-8c4cbe17af76|edge-call-child |Running|agent2|17:11:25.865645
  LINK: 10c7834e... step=1 child=503a3d6e...
  ```

  `docker compose restart agent1` at `17:12:40.925Z` → SIGTERM at `17:12:41.264382Z` → replacement `"agent registered"` `17:12:42.886068Z` → `controller2` `"agent reconcile: failed orphaned run (agent process replaced)"` `17:12:42.894568Z` → parent `Failed 17:12:42.889561`, child `Cancelled 17:12:42.892803`. **Restart issued → child terminal in the DB: 1.968 s.** `agent2` acted on the cancellation at `17:12:45.877601Z` (`"received cancellation signal from master; interrupting run"`), i.e. **4.953 s** from the restart to the child's OS process actually stopping. The final sweep shows all 9 runs terminal, no run left `Running`, and exactly three `agent reconcile` lines — one per restart arm.

  **Part C (I3).** `edge-sideeffect` `1897dd94` held the mutex (`edge-mutex <- 1897dd94... @17:26:30.94374`, `w2-2/partC-mutex.txt`). Restart issued `17:26:33.116Z`; run `Failed 17:26:35.028818` with its step terminalized at `17:26:35.027942`; `mutex_holders` / `named_lock_slots` both read 0 at the very first post-restart sample (`17:26:35.274Z`) and at every sample after. `MarkRunFinished` / `FinishRun` (`internal/store/postgres.go:746-780`) does the status CAS and both lock deletes inside one transaction, so release cannot lag terminalization. The successor is the real proof: `edge-mutex-successor` `cd69dfe9` created `17:26:57.076986` reached `Succeeded 17:26:57.187623` — **0.111 s** — with `acquired-mutex-ok` in its log at `17:26:57.18149Z`.
- **Expected:** `docs/high-availability.md:414-417` (fails every Running run under that ID, locks released, `call:` descendants cascade-cancelled) and I3 (a terminal run holds no locks). Both held exactly as written.
- **Notes:** Recorded because W2-5, W2-7 and the W2 checkpoint all need to know that the cascade *does* fire when the `step_reports.child_run_id` link is present — W2-5's premise is the case where it is absent, and this entry is the control that separates the two. The one caveat carried forward is the step-row inconsistency filed above: the cascade terminalizes the child *run* but not the child's *steps*.

## W2-3 — the stuck-run reaper's `a.id IS NULL` branch has no liveness test at all, so a healthy, heartbeating agent's run is failed as "agent lost" whenever its `agents` row is absent — and the heartbeat the docs say prevents exactly this cannot restore the row
- **Invariant:** I1 (run accounting)
- **Severity:** major
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-3-reaper-boundary.md`, Arm C2 (the reap) and Arm C2b (the self-heal-while-busy measurement). Raw evidence: `w2-3/armC2-setup.txt`, `w2-3/armC2-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armC2-evidence.txt`, `w2-3/armC2b-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armC2b-heartbeats.txt`, `w2-3/armC2b-runid.txt`, `w2-3/armA-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armA-reaper-log.txt`, `w2-3/armA-unblock-rowrestore.txt`, `w2-3/baseline-gate.txt`.
- **Observed:** `edge-longrun` `a307c21c` was claimed by **`agent1` at `18:16:07.902709`** on the plain `test/ha` stack — **no partition, no kill, no fault of any kind on the agent**. At `18:17:01.27-18:17:01.81Z`, with the run at `claim_age = 53.6 s`, its `agents` row was removed with `DELETE FROM agents WHERE id='agent1'` — **the identical statement** `DeleteStaleAgents` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1226`) and `DeleteAgent` (`:1150`, reachable over `DELETE /api/v1/agents/{agentId}` → `handleAgentDeregister`, `internal/controller/api_agents.go:48-55`) execute. The precondition was therefore **induced directly rather than produced by a fault** — a demonstration of the code path, in the sense the W2 plan sanctions for W2-6 Arm C — but the code path reached is the product's, unmodified.

  **17.686 s later the reaper failed the run**: `runs.updated_at = 18:17:19.186064`, with `controller2` logging `{"time":"2026-07-29T18:17:19.189245886Z","level":"WARN","msg":"stuck-run reaper: failed orphaned run (agent lost)","runId":"a307c21c-..."}`. That is `claim_age = 71.28 s`, i.e. **11.283 s past `claimed_at + grace(60s)`** — the grace was the only gate, exactly as `ListStuckRunIDs` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1238-1248`) is written: with `a.id` NULL the `LEFT JOIN` disjunct is satisfied unconditionally and `last_seen_at` is never consulted.

  **`agent1` was demonstrably alive and healthy throughout.** Its log contains **zero** `"agent heartbeat failed"` lines across the window (`grep -c` = 0, `w2-3/armC2-evidence.txt`). Its step kept producing output that the controller **accepted and stored after the run was already Failed**: `tick 73` / `tick 74` at `18:17:21.912134Z` and `tick 75` at `18:17:23.913558Z` — the last accepted stdout line landing **4.727 s after** `runs.updated_at`. The agent learned of the terminal status only from its own status poll, **3.724 s after** the reap: `{"time":"2026-07-29T18:17:22.909773474Z","msg":"master reported run terminal; interrupting run","status":"Failed"}`, then `"run already terminal at master; skipping local outputs/finish"` at `18:17:24.919012Z`.

  **The heartbeat cannot undo the deletion.** `handleAgentHeartbeat` calls `TouchAgent` (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:96`), a bare `UPDATE agents SET last_seen_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1144-1147`): with the row gone it matches **zero rows**, returns no error, and the handler still answers **204**. The row stayed absent for **23.420 s** (`18:17:01.5` → `last_seen_at = 18:17:24.920429`), spanning at least one 15 s heartbeat tick, and was restored only by `UpsertAgentOnClaim`'s `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` (`api_agent.go:150`, `postgres.go:1129-1139`) — i.e. by a **claim poll**. In this particular run the restoring poll was plausibly the one issued from the normal execution slot the reap had just freed: `last_seen_at` came back at `18:17:24.920429`, **1.4 ms** after the agent logged `"run already terminal at master; skipping local outputs/finish"` at `18:17:24.919012Z`. That coincidence is **observed here and nothing is generalized from it** — the freed slot genuinely won this race, but it is not the only claim poll the agent runs; see Arm C2b.

  **Arm C2b — the row self-heals while the agent stays busy, in ≤28.6 s.** Same rig with statement logging on: `agent2` claimed `a70a054a` at `18:52:23.226113`, its `agents` row was deleted at `18:52:24.338116` **with the run `Running`**, and the row was absent at the `18:52:50.41465` sample and back at `18:52:52.980001` — **≤28.642 s** — with the run still `Running` at that sample and at every sample through `18:53:26.179611`, and **no execution slot ever freed** (`w2-3/armC2b-poll.txt`). The restoring write is again `UpsertAgentOnClaim`, issued here by the **detached** claim pool, which polls independently of the normal slots (see Notes). The two `agent2` heartbeats that landed inside the gap (`18:52:30.392`, `18:52:45.392`, `w2-3/armC2b-heartbeats.txt`) did not restore it — the direct proof of the asymmetry. Arm A supplies the third data point: `agent2`'s row, deleted by the real `DeleteStaleAgents` at `18:07:49.053745939Z`, stayed absent for the whole remaining partition (a claim poll that cannot reach the controller cannot upsert) and reappeared **0.16 s** after `nginx-unblock` at `18:11:08.173Z` — restored by the claim poll the instant it could reach the controller, never by a heartbeat.
- **Expected:** Three documented statements are contradicted.
  1. `docs/high-availability.md:371-377` says the heartbeat exists precisely so this cannot happen: it "is **independent of claim polling** — a fully-saturated agent (all concurrency slots busy executing Runs) still sends heartbeats on its own ticker. This matters because `DeleteStaleAgents` ... removes agents whose `last_seen_at` is stale; **before the heartbeat existed, a busy-but-alive agent that wasn't polling for new claims could be misidentified as dead and deleted**." The heartbeat prevents the row from *going* stale; it gives **zero** protection once the row is absent, and the busy-but-alive agent is misidentified as dead exactly as described.
  2. `docs/high-availability.md:536` bounds the failure: an agent that dies mid-run has its run failed "within `staleAfter` (90s) + reaper interval (30s) **of its last heartbeat**". Here the run was failed with the last heartbeat a few seconds old — far inside the promised bound.
  3. `docs/troubleshooting.md:753-758` tells the operator the cause of `stuck-run reaper: failed orphaned run (agent lost)` is that "the agent that claimed the run stopped sending heartbeats (crashed, was killed, or lost network connectivity) and never resumed", and the very next section (`:779-784`) uses that log line as the **discriminator** between the slow stale-heartbeat path and the fast heartbeat-reconcile path. Both are wrong here: the line was emitted for an agent whose heartbeat never went stale, so an operator following the guide investigates agent health and finds nothing.
- **Notes:** Root cause: the reaper treats *absence of an inventory row* as *proof of agent death*, and the heartbeat — the one write the agent makes unconditionally on its own ticker — cannot recreate the row. The only write path that recreates it is `UpsertAgentOnClaim`, which `handleAgentClaim` executes on **every** claim poll, before it branches on `kind` and before any long-poll wait (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:150`).
  - **The exposure is a bounded race window, and an earlier version of this entry stated the mechanism wrongly.** It said the restoring write "is the claim poll, which a busy `--max-concurrent 1` agent does not run". That is false. `Agent.Run` starts a **detached claim pool of 16 slots by default, entirely separate from `--max-concurrent`** (`internal/agent/agent.go:315-321`: `d := a.MaxDetachedConcurrent`, `d == 0 → 16`), and every one of those slots loops on `ClaimDetached(ctx, id, "30s", labels)` (`agent.go:411-415`, `internal/agent/client.go:189-200`, which appends `&kind=detached` to the same claim path). A busy agent therefore claim-polls continuously, and a deleted row is re-inserted within **at most one claim-poll cycle — ~30 s with the default 30 s long-poll timeout**. Measured: **≤28.642 s in Arm C2b, with the run still `Running`** and no slot freed.
  - **So the defect fires when a reaper sweep lands inside that window**, which is exactly what Arm C2 engineered: row deleted at `claim_age = 53.6 s`, eligibility (`claimed_at + 60 s`) reached **6.4 s** later, sweep reaped at **17.7 s**, row restored at **23.4 s** — the reap beat the self-heal by 5.7 s. A ~30 s exposure against a 30 s sweep interval is not a narrow race: any deletion landing on a run already past `claimed_at + 60 s` has roughly even odds of being reaped before the agent can answer for itself, and the run is silently lost when it is.
  - **It is unbounded only when the detached pool is switched off** — `--max-detached-concurrent` set negative (`agent.go:319-321`: `d < 0 → 0`) — **and** every normal slot is busy, because then nothing calls the claim endpoint at all and the row stays absent until a slot frees. `test/ha` passes neither concurrency flag (`test/ha/docker-compose.ha.yaml:103-117`, `:124-138`), so this rig ran with `--max-concurrent` at its default 1 (`cmd/unified-cd-agent/main.go:71-75`) and the detached pool at its default 16.
  - The `docs/high-availability.md:393` rationale ("`staleAfter` (90s) is intentionally well under `DeleteStaleAgents`'s 5-minute threshold ... the `LEFT JOIN` handles the rarer case where the agent row was already deleted first") is correct about the nesting — measured here at **210.022 s** between the run going terminal and the row being deleted (Arm A) — but the nesting is what leaves this branch untested in normal operation, not what makes it safe. Fixes, in increasing thoroughness: make the heartbeat an upsert like the claim path; or gate the null branch on the same `staleAfter` clock the other branch uses.
  - **Reachability without a direct DELETE — the long-outage story is refuted; the drain story is the real one. Neither was demonstrated live here** (Arm C2 induced the DELETE directly). A ">5-minute outage lets `DeleteStaleAgents` remove the row first" path does **not** reach this defect: a partition long enough to age `last_seen_at` past 5 minutes has already tripped the ordinary stale-heartbeat branch at ~2 minutes, so by the time the row is deleted the agent has no `Running` runs left to expose — Arm A measures that nesting at **210.022 s** — and on the heal the row returns in **0.16 s** (`w2-3/armA-unblock-rowrestore.txt`), before any new run can be exposed, because `UpsertAgentOnClaim` runs earlier in the same request than the `claimed_at` write. The genuinely natural trigger is narrower and more concrete: `Agent.Run` calls `Client.Deregister` **unconditionally at the end of drain** (`internal/agent/agent.go:344-350`), and `DeleteAgent` is a bare `DELETE FROM agents WHERE id = $1` with no ownership, generation or fencing check (`internal/store/postgres.go:1149-1152`). **Any topology in which an old process's drain outlasts a successor's registration under the same agent ID — an overlapping restart, a start-before-stop rollout, a duplicate-ID sibling shutting down — therefore deletes a row the live process now owns, with no partition and no operator action anywhere.** The deregister *endpoint* is agent-authenticated to its own ID (`internal/controller/server.go:246`, `agentAuth` + `requireAgentPathIdentity`), which is precisely why a same-ID sibling is the plausible actor rather than a third party. That configuration is **W2-7's**; that scenario should test this interaction explicitly.
  - A second, unexercised consequence of the same missing row: `ListUnclaimableQueuedRuns` (`postgres.go:1275-1286`) also decides eligibility with `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM agents ...)`, so while the row is absent a live agent is invisible to the queued-run reaper too (code-read, not observed).

## W2-3 — observation: a heartbeat for a deleted `agents` row is a zero-row UPDATE that still answers 204, so the agent, its logs and the controller all show a healthy agent that the reaper simultaneously treats as gone
- **Invariant:** none violated — this is a **diagnosability** defect. I5 (bounded recovery) is cited only as the nearest available bucket, and the citation is deliberately weak: with the ≤28.642 s self-heal now measured (Arm C2b), recovery of the row **was** bounded, so I5 is not the property that failed. What failed is that nothing anywhere reports the interval.
- **Severity:** minor (observation; it is the mechanism behind the major above)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-3-reaper-boundary.md`, Arms C2 and A. Raw evidence: `w2-3/armC2-evidence.txt`, `w2-3/armC2-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armA-unblock-rowrestore.txt`.
- **Observed:** For the **23.420 s** the `agent1` row was absent (Arm C2), nothing anywhere reported a problem: the agent logged no `"agent heartbeat failed"` (count **0**), `POST /heartbeat` kept returning 204 because `TouchAgent` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1144-1147`, called at `internal/controller/api_agent.go:96`) discards `RowsAffected`, and the agent went on streaming step output the controller accepted (`tick 75` at `18:17:23.913558Z`). The only observable was `GET /api/v1/agents` omitting the agent — a negative signal an operator has to be looking for. The row also came back silently: no log line marks `UpsertAgentOnClaim` re-inserting it.
- **Expected:** Not covered by any doc. `docs/high-availability.md:371-377` describes the heartbeat as what keeps a busy agent from being "misidentified as dead" without saying it cannot re-register one.
- **Notes:** A one-line change — `TouchAgent` reporting `RowsAffected == 0`, and the handler either logging it or answering 404 so the agent re-registers — turns a silent, invisible state into a loud one. Filed separately from the major because it is independently actionable, and because it is the same class as W1-6's `last_authenticated_at` finding: a write path that is silently a no-op.

## W2-3 — observation: the reaper's two conjuncts, measured — the 60s claim grace can never bind in the heartbeat-loss branch, and is the *only* gate in the deleted-row branch
- **Invariant:** none violated (I1 and I5 both held on the natural path). Recorded as the boundary measurement the scenario exists to produce.
- **Severity:** minor (observation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-3-reaper-boundary.md`, Arms A, B1, B2, C0. Raw evidence: `w2-3/armA-claim-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armA-block.txt`, `w2-3/armA-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armA-reaper-log.txt`, `w2-3/armB2-setup.txt`, `w2-3/armB2-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armD0-reap-poll.txt`, `w2-3/baseline-gate.txt`.
- **Observed:**
  - **Arm A — the natural path; `staleAfter` binds.** `edge-longrun` `cbe891f1` claimed by `agent2` at `18:02:30.766826`; `agent2` blocked at nginx at `18:02:44.369Z`; its `last_seen_at` froze at `18:02:29.785911` and never moved again. Eligibility `max(claimed_at+60s, last_seen_at+90s) = max(18:03:30.767, 18:03:59.786)` → **the 90 s staleness term binds**; the run was failed at `runs.updated_at = 18:04:19.031807` (`controller2`, `18:04:19.035134692Z`). **Reap latency past eligibility: 19.246 s.** Last heartbeat → run failed: **109.246 s**, inside the `90 s + 30 s = 120 s` bound `docs/high-availability.md:536` promises. The `step_reports` row was terminalized **2.2 ms** before the run (`18:04:19.029584`). `mutex_holders` / `named_lock_slots` read 0 throughout — `edge-longrun` declares no mutex, so **I3 here is a null result, not a pass**.
  - **B1 — why the grace can never bind on that path.** Measured `last_seen_at - claimed_at = **-0.980915 s**` at the claim: `UpsertAgentOnClaim` fires at the *start* of a claim request that long-polls up to 60 s (`maxClaimTimeout`, `internal/controller/api_agent.go:129`), so the claim does not refresh liveness at claim time; the 15 s heartbeat (`internal/agent/heartbeat.go:10`) is what keeps the gap small. Since a live agent keeps `last_seen_at >= claimed_at - 15 s`, `last_seen_at + 90s >= claimed_at + 75s > claimed_at + 60s` **always**. The general inequality is **derived** from the 15 s interval; only the single `-0.98 s` sample is measured.
  - **B2 — forcing the inverted ordering (direct DB mutation, not a naturally occurring state).** `edge-longrun` `1cf20d42` claimed by `agent2` at `18:12:21.099946`, blocked at nginx, then `UPDATE agents SET last_seen_at = NOW() - interval '95 seconds'` applied at `18:12:23.93`. Staleness was satisfied from `18:12:18.406`, yet the run stayed `Running` through every sample with `hb_age > 90` until `claim_age` crossed 60 — last `Running` sample `claim_age=85.75, hb_age=178.45` — and was failed at `18:13:49.160548`, i.e. **28.061 s past `claimed_at + 60s`** and **90.755 s after** staleness alone would have allowed it. **The grace conjunct binds exactly as `ListStuckRunIDs` is written.**
  - **C0 — `DeleteStaleAgents` on the natural path, and the nesting.** With `agent2` still blocked, the `agents` row was present at the `18:07:46.011713` sample and absent at `18:07:49.576227`; `controller2` logged `{"msg":"deleteStaleAgents","deleted":1}` at `18:07:49.053745939Z` — **19.268 s past** `last_seen_at + 300 s`, on a 1-minute tick. The run had been terminal for **210.022 s** by then, so on the natural path the `a.id IS NULL` branch never reaps anything, exactly as `docs/high-availability.md:393` says. `SELECT '5m0s'::interval` returns `00:05:00`, so the Go-duration string `DeleteStaleAgents` passes into `NOW() - $1::interval` (`postgres.go:1226-1228`) is valid — checked because a failure there would have meant the job had never run at all.
  - **Four reap latencies past eligibility, one per arm: 19.246 s (A), 28.061 s (B2), 11.283 s (C2), 8.013 s (D0)** — all under the 30 s nominal interval, three of the four above the `interval / N` figure the W2 plan **originally** said to budget against — that rule has since been corrected in place at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-30-edge-case-campaign-w2.md:47` (see the next entry).
- **Expected:** `docs/high-availability.md:380-395` documents the predicate, both conjuncts and the nesting, and every one of those statements held; `:536`'s 120 s bound held at 109.246 s.
- **Notes:** The consequence for later waves: the 60 s grace is dead weight on the heartbeat path and is the *entire* gate on the deleted-row path, which is why the major above is a 60-second exposure rather than a 90-second one. B2's backdating is a direct DB write and is labelled as such; nothing in the conclusion depends on the mutation except the ordering it forces.

## W2-3 — observation: correction to the W2 plan's `interval / N` rule — the per-tick jobs' lock acquisitions are *clustered within ~10 ms*, not spread, so reap latency is bounded by the nominal 30 s and only DB load scales with N
- **Invariant:** none. Recorded because a plan fact established by W2-1 was applied incorrectly and would have mis-budgeted this scenario and W2-4.
- **Severity:** minor (observation / methodological correction)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-3-reaper-boundary.md`, Arm D0 instrumentation. Raw evidence: `w2-3/armD0-sweeps.txt`, derived from `w2-3/armD0-postgres-full.log` (a `log_statement='all'` capture with `log_line_prefix='%m [%p] h=%h '`, reverted at teardown).
- **Observed:** Every execution of `ListStuckRunIDs`' SQL over a 210 s window, keyed by client IP: **8 clusters, 17 executions**. Within a cluster the executions are **0.002-0.011 s** apart; between clusters the gap is **29.987-30.001 s**. There is no execution anywhere in between. Two clusters had three winners (`18:24:19.215 / .223 / .225`), the rest two. So W2-1's 2.15 acquisitions per nominal interval is confirmed as a *count*, but the acquisitions are simultaneous, not interleaved: the three replicas' 30 s tickers share a phase because they boot together under `docker compose up`, so the 2-3 winners of a cluster race within ~10 ms and the next opportunity is a full 30 s later.
- **Expected:** The W2 plan's Verified-facts block states as a consequence for later tasks: "any reaper-latency or DB-load budget must use the effective cadence `interval / N` ... the stuck-run reaper swept every ~13.3s here, not 30s. **This tightens reap latency** (so a boundary test that budgets a full 30s tick will over-wait)". The load half is right; the latency half is wrong on this rig. The four reap latencies measured in this scenario (19.246, 28.061, 11.283, 8.013 s) are consistent with a uniform wait over a **30 s** cycle and inconsistent with a 13.3 s one.
- **Notes:** This is rig-shaped in the same way W2-1's 2.15 was, and for the same reason: simultaneous replica boot. A cluster whose replicas start at staggered times (rolling deploy, k8s rollout) would spread the phases and genuinely shorten latency toward `interval / N`; a `compose up` cluster will not. **Rule for W2-4 and later: `interval / N` for query load, the nominal `interval` for worst-case latency — and say which you are budgeting.** The instrument is W2-1's statement log filtered to the *job's own SQL* rather than to `pg_try_advisory_lock`: the acquisition count and the execution timing answer different questions. **Propagated, not just recorded:** the W2 plan's Verified-facts block now carries this correction in place under a `CORRECTED after W2-3 execution` banner (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-30-edge-case-campaign-w2.md:47`), so Task 5 (W2-4) reads the corrected rule at the point of use rather than the superseded `interval / N` latency budget at `:46`.

## W2-3 — `failOrphanedRun`'s crash window is **1 ms** wide and nothing re-drives a cascade lost inside it — measured, but NOT reproduced live in 10 attempts
- **Invariant:** I1 (run accounting) — the consequence would be a `call:` descendant left uncancelled after its parent is reaped.
- **Severity:** minor as filed here (the defect is real and the window is measured, but the failure was **not observed**; severity would be major if reproduced). Recorded so the argument and its cost are on the record rather than as a claim of an observed defect.
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-3-reaper-boundary.md`, Arms D0 (window measurement + no-kill control) and D1 (10 attempts). Raw evidence: `w2-3/armD0-window.txt`, `w2-3/armD0-postgres-full.log.gz`, `w2-3/armD0-setup.txt`, `w2-3/armD0-linkpoll.txt`, `w2-3/armD0-reap-poll.txt`, `w2-3/armD1-attempts.txt`, `w2-3/armD1-run.log`, `w2-3/armD1.sh`.
- **Observed:**
  - **The window, measured from the Postgres statement log** (`log_statement='all'`, `log_line_prefix='%m [%p] h=%h '`), for the natural reap of `edge-call-parent` `c0ce2fbd` with its linked child `6053e68a`, all on one backend (pid 677, `172.20.0.3`), `w2-3/armD0-window.txt`:

    ```
    18:22:19.189  SELECT r.id FROM runs r LEFT JOIN agents a ...   (ListStuckRunIDs, $1=90 $2=60)
    18:22:19.190  UPDATE step_reports SET status='Failed' ...      (MarkRunStepsInterrupted, stuckrun_reaper.go:81)
    18:22:19.192  begin
    18:22:19.193  UPDATE runs SET status='Failed' ...              (MarkRunFinished, :84)
    18:22:19.193  DELETE FROM mutex_holders WHERE run_id = $1
    18:22:19.194  UPDATE named_lock_slots SET run_id = NULL ...
    18:22:19.194  commit                                           <-- window opens
    18:22:19.195  SELECT child_run_id::text FROM step_reports ...   <-- window closes (cancelDescendantRuns, :88)
    18:22:19.196  begin / UPDATE runs SET status='Cancelled' (child) / commit
    18:22:19.197  SELECT child_run_id::text ... (child, BFS)
    ```

    **The crash window is `commit` → the first child SELECT: 1 ms** at Postgres's log resolution. The whole `failOrphanedRun` sequence is **8 ms** (`.189` → `.197`). Parent and child terminal timestamps agreed: parent `Failed 18:22:19.192464`, child `Cancelled 18:22:19.196061`, **3.597 ms** apart.
  - **The no-kill control passed** — with no injection at all, the parent was reaped and the child was `Cancelled` in the same sequence, so the detector (parent `Failed` **and** child not `Cancelled`) distinguishes a hit from a broken harness.
  - **Ten attempts, zero hits, zero skipped** (`w2-3/armD1-attempts.txt`). Each attempt built a fresh linked `edge-call-parent`/`edge-call-child` pair, gated on `step_reports.child_run_id` existing, collapsed eligibility to `claimed_at + 60s` by `nginx-block` + removing the claiming agent's row (so the reap landed on a predictable sweep), then fired one `docker kill -s SIGKILL` at `controller1` aimed at the predicted sweep instant. Every attempt ended `Failed|Cancelled` — cascade completed. The 11 measured parent→child cascade deltas (D0 plus the 10 attempts) span **1.984-4.140 ms**.
  - **The near misses are the informative part.** In **9 of the 10** attempts the reap instant fell *inside* the `docker kill` round trip — the SIGKILL was issued **0.400-0.677 s before** the reap and the CLI returned **0.023-0.271 s after** it, so the signal was in flight across the window every time and never landed in it. (Attempt 1 was the outlier at +11.426 s, before the sweep phase was locked in.) **`docker kill` round trip, recomputed directly from the capture** (`w2-3/armD1-attempts.txt`, `returned` − `issued` per attempt, i.e. `T1 − T0` at `armD1.sh:54-56`): **0.636-0.707 s** across the ten attempts. **No `docker start` figure is claimed** — `armD1.sh` takes `T1` at `:56`, *before* the `docker start` at `:57`, so the restart was never timed. An earlier version of this entry quoted `0.642-0.657 s` for the kill and `0.468-0.481 s` for the start; neither traces to any capture in `w2-3/` and both have been replaced by the line above.
  - **Derived, not measured:** with a ~0.67 s uncertainty in signal delivery against a 1 ms window, the per-attempt hit probability is ~`1/670` ≈ **0.15%**, i.e. ~1.5% over ten attempts. **Container-level fault injection is roughly three orders of magnitude too coarse for this window**, and no amount of repetition within a reasonable budget changes that. Two finer instruments were considered and rejected: an in-container `kill -9 1` (the kernel ignores SIGKILL sent to PID 1 from inside its own PID namespace, and the controller is PID 1 under `ENTRYPOINT ["/controller"]`), and a server-side `pg_sleep` + `pg_terminate_backend` spray at the reaper's pooled backend (would exercise `ListChildRunIDs`' *error* branch rather than a crash, and needs the same millisecond timing).
- **Expected:** `stuckrun_reaper.go:76-90` performs `MarkRunStepsInterrupted` → `MarkRunFinished` → `cancelDescendantRuns` with **no transaction spanning them**, and `ListStuckRunIDs` (`postgres.go:1238-1248`) selects only `status = 'Running'`, so a run failed at `:84` is never re-listed. Nothing else re-drives the cascade either: **this was checked and settled** — `handleAgentFinishRun` (`api_agent.go:608-627`) calls `cancelDescendantRuns` **only when `FinishRun` returned `updated == true`**, and a reaped parent is already terminal, so the executing agent's later finish report takes the `!updated` branch and returns without cascading. The startup/heartbeat reconciles list only `Running` runs under the agent, which the reaped parent no longer is. A crash (or, identically, a transient DB error — `cancelDescendantRuns` at `api_runs.go:390-412` logs and continues on `ListChildRunIDs` failure) inside that 1 ms therefore leaves the descendant permanently uncancelled, and it runs to completion as an orphan.
- **Notes:** **Not reproduced live — this entry is a code-read argument plus a measurement of the window, and makes no claim that the failure was observed.** What the measurement adds over the code read is the size: 1 ms out of a 30 s sweep, which is both why it has never been seen in practice and why chasing it with `docker kill` is futile. The cheap fix is to widen `FinishRun`'s existing transaction to cover the descendant walk, or to make the cascade re-drivable by selecting reaped parents whose descendants are still active. The related W2-2 finding is the same shape one level down: `MarkRunStepsInterrupted` inside `failOrphanedRun` is scoped to its own `runID`, so even a *successful* cascade leaves the descendants' step rows `Running`. **Carry-forward:** if a later wave wants this window reproduced, it needs a debugger-level or fault-injection-library instrument, not container orchestration — worth deciding explicitly rather than re-running the same 10 attempts.

## W2-3 — resolved branch-internal asset bug: `inject.sh nginx-unblock` demanded a service argument it never used, so the documented invocation exited 1 under `set -eu` and silently left the partition in place
- **Classification:** **Not a violation and not an observation — and therefore not counted in either W2 tally.** It is a defect in this campaign's **own** assets, found and fixed inside this branch; no product code, schema or documented contract is implicated, and nothing about it is still true at HEAD. Filed under the third bucket the W1 checkpoint established (`FINDINGS.md:278` for the precedent entry, `:487` for the rule), and reported outside the violation and observation counts in the same way.
- **Invariant:** N/A — test-tooling correctness, not a runtime invariant.
- **Severity:** n/a (resolved within the branch; see **Resolution** below).
- **Repro (as it stood at `b3c278e`, the commit that shipped the tool):** from `test/ha/`, `sh ../edgecase/tools/inject.sh nginx-unblock`.
- **Observed:** exit status **1** with `inject.sh: line 12: 2: service name required` and no nginx reload. *(The failure was hit live during the W2-3 run but its output was not captured to scratchpad; the exit code and message quoted here were re-derived after the fact by running the pre-fix blob — `git show b3c278e:test/edgecase/tools/inject.sh > inject-old.sh && sh inject-old.sh nginx-unblock` — which aborts at `:12` before any `docker` call, so no stack is needed to reproduce it.)* `inject.sh` bound `svc="${2:?service name required}"` **unconditionally at `:12`**, before dispatching on `$cmd`, while the `nginx-unblock` branch (`:29-32`) clears the whole blocklist (`: > /etc/nginx/blocklist/deny.conf && nginx -s reload`) and **never references `$svc`**. Under the file's own `set -eu` the parameter expansion aborts the script. The failure mode is the dangerous kind rather than the loud kind: a teardown or heal step written as `inject.sh nginx-unblock || true` (which is how a cleanup line is naturally written, and how the W2-3 teardown block was written) swallows the exit code, so the operator sees no error and the agent stays partitioned into whatever runs next. W1-5 had already hit this and worked around it in prose rather than fixing it — `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-5-oneway-partition-zombie.md:297` documents the invocation as `inject.sh nginx-unblock <any-service-arg>   # unblocks all`, i.e. a required argument that is explicitly discarded — so the defect survived a whole wave as a documented wart.
- **Expected:** the command's own usage string at `:11` lists `nginx-unblock` alongside commands that genuinely need a service, and the tool is the campaign's only documented heal instrument for the one-way partition every W1/W2 partition arm depends on. A heal that silently no-ops is worse than one that fails loudly, because every subsequent measurement in the scenario is then taken against a stack that is still partitioned.
- **Resolution (fixed in this branch, commit `a7ae25a`):** the binding is now dispatched on the command — `case "$cmd" in nginx-unblock) svc="${2:-}" ;; *) svc="${2:?service name required}" ;; esac` (`test/edgecase/tools/inject.sh:14-17`) — so `nginx-unblock` takes no argument and every other command still requires one. The usage string at `:11` was updated to `[service]`. **Callers to update if you copy from an older runbook:** `w1-5-oneway-partition-zombie.md:297` still documents the old `<any-service-arg>` workaround; it is harmless (the argument is now simply ignored) but no longer necessary.
- **Notes:** The methodological lesson is the same one the W1-4 `spec.mutex` entry records, in a different guise: a campaign asset that is only ever exercised on the *happy* path of a scenario can carry a defect through an entire wave, because the arm that would expose it (teardown, heal) is the arm whose failure is deliberately suppressed. A cheap standing rule for later waves: **never write `|| true` on an injection or heal command without also asserting the post-condition** — here, one `curl` through the LB from the blocked agent's IP, or one `docker compose exec nginx cat /etc/nginx/blocklist/deny.conf`, would have caught it on first use.

## W2-4 — the queued-run reaper fails a run that a live, eligible agent has already claimed and started executing: its unclaimability list is never re-validated, and the stale window is the whole sweep loop — 475 ms for a 161-run backlog, against 4 ms for a single run
- **Invariant:** I1 (run accounting)
- **Severity:** major
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-4-queued-reaper.md`, Part B5 (backlog amplification), driven by `w2-4/partB-backlog.sh`. Raw evidence: `w2-4/partB-bk1-run.txt`, `w2-4/partB-bk1-evidence.txt`, `w2-4/partB-bk1-window.txt`, `w2-4/partB-bk1-second.log` (every logged statement in the second `22:43:59`), `w2-4/partB-bk1-poll.txt`, `w2-4/partB-bk1-ids.txt`, `w2-4/partB-sweeps.txt`, `w2-4/partB-ph5a-window.txt` (the single-run window), `w2-4/pgtail3.log.gz` (the `log_statement='all'` capture the two window files are cut from, `log_line_prefix='%m [%p] h=%h '`, reverted at teardown — `w2-4/partB-stmtlog-on.txt`, `w2-4/partB-stmtlog-off.txt`).
- **Observed:** With `UNIFIED_QUEUED_RUN_GRACE=30s` and both agents cleanly stopped (so both `agents` rows were deleted — see the observation entries below), 250 `edge-tick` runs were submitted between `22:43:13.200015` and `22:43:38.458705`, all reaching `Queued`. `agent1` was then returned with `docker start` timed to land ~0.25 s into the next sweep, using the measured sweep grid and the measured 0.400 s container-start-to-registration latency (`w2-4/partB-calibration2.txt`). **One attempt, one hit.** The interleaving, entirely from the Postgres statement log (`w2-4/partB-bk1-window.txt`), all times on the DB clock:

  ```
  22:43:59.462   ListUnclaimableQueuedRuns SELECT   (pid 36571, h=172.20.0.3)  <-- the liveness check
  22:43:59.469   UPDATE runs -> Failed  1c9d9173...  (first of the batch)
  22:43:59.485   UpsertAgent INSERT: $1='agent1' $4='{kind:linux}' $6='{native,container}'  <-- agent live
  22:43:59.494   claimed_at: agent1 claims b77028e6, status -> Running
  22:43:59.499   step_reports step 0 'tick' status='Running', started_at 22:43:59.49922
  22:43:59.501   INSERT INTO logs: "run failed: no eligible agent available to claim it (requires agent labels: kind:linux)"
  22:43:59.502   UPDATE runs -> Failed  b77028e6...   (runs.updated_at = 22:43:59.502173)
  22:43:59.937   UPDATE runs -> Failed  3c9d600a...   (last of the batch, 161 runs)
  ```

  So run `b77028e6-0c4f-4dde-b30a-eb4a172f09b4` was **failed 8.0 ms after a live, label-matching agent claimed it and 3.0 ms after that agent's step was already recorded `Running`**, with the reason "no eligible agent available to claim it". `agent1`'s `agents` row had existed for **17.2 ms** at the moment of the failing UPDATE, and the SELECT that authorised the failure was **40.2 ms** stale. `controller2` logged `{"time":"2026-07-29T22:43:59.504067378Z","level":"WARN","msg":"queued-run reaper: failed unclaimable queued run","runId":"b77028e6-...","agentSelector":["kind:linux"]}`.

  **The agent then kept executing the run for six more seconds and the controller accepted its output.** `w2-4/partB-bk1-evidence.txt` holds the run's log rows: `tick 1`/`tick 2` at `22:44:01.505776`, `tick 3`/`tick 4` at `22:44:03.506423`, `tick 5` at `22:44:05.506653` — the last accepted stdout landing **6.005 s after** `runs.updated_at`, each batch answered `204` by a controller (`controller2`/`controller3` access lines in the same capture). The agent discovered the terminal status only from its own status poll and killed the step at `22:44:04.502983` (`unified-cd: step "tick" failed to execute: context canceled`), **3.001 s after** the reap. The `step_reports` row is left `status='Running'` with a NULL `ended_at` under a `Failed` run — the same dangling-step shape already filed at `FINDINGS.md:587` (W2-2) and `:330` (W1-5); **cross-referenced, not re-filed**.

  **The window is the reaper's whole loop, and it scales with the backlog.** `runQueuedRunReaperOnce` (`internal/controller/queuedrun_reaper.go:59-79`) takes the entire reapable set in one SELECT and then iterates `AppendLog` -> `MarkRunFinished` -> `cancelDescendantRuns` per run, re-reading nothing. Measured at both ends: with a **single** reapable run the SELECT-to-terminalizing-UPDATE distance is **4 ms** (`22:38:29.333` → `22:38:29.337`, `w2-4/partB-ph5a-window.txt`); with **161** it is **475 ms** (`22:43:59.462` → `22:43:59.937`), i.e. **~2.93 ms per run (derived: 468 ms / 160 intervals)**. A side effect worth noting from the same capture: that 475 ms hold is long enough to actually exclude the other replicas — the `22:43:59` sweep cluster contains **one** execution, where all 37 other clusters in `w2-4/partB-sweeps.txt` contain two or three.
- **Expected:** Two published statements are contradicted.
  1. `docs/high-availability.md:89` states the job's contract as failing "Queued runs that **no live agent can claim** within `UNIFIED_QUEUED_RUN_GRACE`". A run that a live agent has already claimed is the exact complement of that set. `docs/high-availability.md:352-356` frames the whole feature around a *full* agent outage; the failure here happens precisely as the outage **ends**, which is the one moment the feature is supposed to stop acting.
  2. `docs/troubleshooting.md:32-73` (`Run stays Queued forever`) and `:74-130` both present a run's `Queued`→terminal transition as governed by whether an agent can claim it. Nothing anywhere says a claimed, executing run can be failed by the queue reaper, and the run's own log says the opposite of what happened.
- **Notes:** Root cause is a read-then-write with no re-validation, plus a CAS too weak to catch it. `MarkRunFinished`/`FinishRun` (`internal/store/postgres.go:746-780`) guards only `WHERE id = $2 AND status NOT IN ('Succeeded','Failed','Cancelled')` — it refuses to overwrite a **terminal** run but happily overwrites a `Running` one, so the claim that happened 8 ms earlier is invisible to it. **I1's literal clause held** — the run reached `Failed` exactly once; the agent's own subsequent `FinishRun(Failed)` call (`internal/agent/orchestrator.go:788`, fired after the step's `context canceled` failure) hits the same CAS and no-ops because the row is already terminal (confirmed by code read, not captured directly in the evidence excerpt). The violation is on the documented-contract limb instead: the run reached a terminal state a live, claiming agent had already superseded, contradicting `docs/high-availability.md:89` and `:349-356` (cited under Expected below). Kept as **I1, major** — same invariant-attribution convention W1-5 used (`FINDINGS.md:484`).
  - **Nothing caps the batch, which is why this window has no ceiling.** `ListUnclaimableQueuedRuns` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1276-1286`) carries no `LIMIT`, and the sweep loop that consumes it (`queuedrun_reaper.go:64`, `for _, ref := range refs`) has no batch cap either — it iterates the entire reapable set returned by the one SELECT. That absence is *why* the window scales with backlog size rather than being bounded at some fixed N.
  - **Cheap fixes, in increasing thoroughness:** the reaper needs its own **reaper-scoped store method** — a CAS on `status = 'Queued'` (not a change to the shared `FinishRun`/`MarkRunFinished`, which also serves the agent finish path, cancel, and the stuck-run reaper — see the correction below on why tightening `FinishRun` itself would break every legitimate `Running`→`Succeeded` transition). Per the mutex/named-lock exposure noted above, that new method must **also gate the two lock releases (`mutex_holders` DELETE, `named_lock_slots` UPDATE) on the CAS result**, since `FinishRun` currently issues both unconditionally regardless of whether the UPDATE actually matched a row. Short of that: re-evaluate the unclaimability predicate per run inside the loop; or bound the batch size and re-SELECT.
  - **Why the amplification is realistic rather than contrived.** The documented trigger for this job is "a *full* k8s-agent outage (for example a node-pool upgrade taking every replica down)" (`docs/high-availability.md:349-356`). A full agent outage of any length *is* a queue backlog, and the outage ending *is* agents re-registering. 161 runs is a small backlog by CI standards; at ~2.93 ms/run a 10,000-run backlog gives a ~29 s window, i.e. essentially the whole sweep interval. **The 161-run figure is measured; the 10,000-run extrapolation is derived from the per-run cost and was not observed.**
  - **What the reaper's predicate does and does not get wrong.** The `NOT EXISTS` conjunct (`internal/store/postgres.go:1275-1286`) is correct and does protect a run whose agent registers *before* the sweep — Part B trial `ph1a` confirms it live: the `agents` row appeared at `22:40:28.375794`, the sweep fired at `22:40:29.376`, and the run was untouched (`w2-4/partB-ph1a-run.txt`). The defect is purely that the answer is cached across the loop.
  - **Related to but distinct from W2-3's major** (`FINDINGS.md:687`). That one is the *stuck*-run reaper treating a missing `agents` row as proof of death. This one is the *queued*-run reaper treating a 40 ms-old liveness answer as current. `FINDINGS.md:710` already flagged that `ListUnclaimableQueuedRuns` shares the `EXISTS(... agents ...)` liveness idiom and predicted an unexercised second limb; this entry exercises a *different* weakness in the same query, so the two do not merge.
  - **Not established (two limbs):**
    1. Whether `cancelDescendantRuns` (called at `queuedrun_reaper.go:77` for every reaped run) can cascade-cancel a *descendant* the returning agent has claimed. `edge-tick` has no `call:` step, so no descendant existed here. The mechanism would be the same stale-list read and the blast radius larger; it needs `edge-call-parent` and its own arm.
    2. **The larger half of the same race: `FinishRun`'s two lock releases are unconditional, not gated on the CAS.** `FinishRun` (`internal/store/postgres.go:746-780`) computes `updated = tag.RowsAffected() > 0` at `:766` but then unconditionally `DELETE`s `mutex_holders` (`:769`) and clears `named_lock_slots` (`:774`) regardless of whether the UPDATE actually matched — those releases are not gated on `updated`. Part C of this scenario observed the same reaper "releasing the mutex the victim had itself just acquired" (`FINDINGS.md:875`), so the identical stale-list race against a run that is **holding a mutex or named-lock slot**, not merely `Running`, would free that lock out from under the now-claimed, executing run, opening a window (bounded by the agent's ~3 s cancel poll, as measured above) for a successor to acquire the same mutex while the original holder is still mid-step. That is an **I3/I2 exposure with a larger blast radius than the `cancelDescendantRuns` limb above. Not observed here** — `edge-tick` carries no mutex — it would need `sideeffect`/`mutex-hog` (or a named-lock job) present in the reaped batch, its own arm.

## W2-4 — observation: the queued-run reaper is the only reaper in the campaign that tells the operator why, appending its reason to the run's own log before failing it — but the reason names the label selector even when labels were never the problem
- **Invariant:** none violated by the good half. The misleading half is a **diagnosability** defect; I5 (bounded recovery) is cited only as the nearest bucket and the citation is deliberately weak — recovery was bounded, what failed is the explanation.
- **Severity:** minor (observation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-4-queued-reaper.md`, Part A. Raw evidence: `w2-4/armA-logs.txt`, `w2-4/armA-poll.txt`, `w2-4/armA-transitions.txt`, `w2-4/armD-surfaces.txt`, `w2-4/partB-bk1-evidence.txt`.
- **Observed:** The good half works exactly as the code promises. `runQueuedRunReaperOnce` appends a system log row at step index **-1** *before* failing the run (`internal/controller/queuedrun_reaper.go:64-72`), and it reaches the read API unchanged: `GET /api/v1/runs/dbed9313-.../logs` returned

  ```json
  [{"seq":2,"stepIndex":-1,"stream":"stderr","timestamp":"2026-07-29T19:42:55.617Z",
    "line":"run failed: no eligible agent available to claim it (requires agent labels: kind:linux)"}]
  ```

  with the matching `logs` row in the DB and a `WARN queued-run reaper: failed unclaimable queued run` on `controller2` at `19:42:55.623781413Z` (`w2-4/armA-logs.txt`). **This is the direct counterexample to the two entries this campaign has already filed about reaps that leave no operator-visible reason** — `FINDINGS.md:652` (W2-2: a run failed by the agent-replacement reconcile carries no reason) and `FINDINGS.md:712` (W2-3, the stuck-run reaper's diagnosability). Worth recording as the pattern the other reapers should copy.
  - **The misleading half.** The `(requires agent labels: ...)` suffix is emitted whenever `ref.AgentSelector` is non-empty (`queuedrun_reaper.go:66-68`), regardless of why nothing could claim the run — and the selector is the one thing that was satisfied in both cases observed here. (a) `edge-podcap-job` run `f9ae8786-...` required capability `pod`; both live agents carried `kind:linux`; when they went away it was failed with `run failed: no eligible agent available to claim it (requires agent labels: kind:linux)` at `19:42:25.599387`, pointing the operator at a label that was never wrong. (b) In the major above, the same line was written onto a run a `kind:linux` agent had claimed 8 ms earlier.
- **Expected:** `docs/troubleshooting.md:104-112` is explicit that the two failure halves are meant to be distinguishable — `no registered agent provides capability [...]` versus `no registered agent matches labels [...]` — and `serveJobSchedulability` already computes exactly that discrimination (`internal/controller/schedulability.go:82-95`, `reasonNoAgent`). The reaper writes the label variant unconditionally instead of reusing it.
- **Notes:** The fix is small and already written elsewhere: phrase the reaper's reason with `reasonNoAgent`'s logic, or simply drop the parenthetical when some live agent does satisfy the selector. Note the operator cannot recover the truth from the message alone — seeing `(requires agent labels: kind:linux)` on a run whose agents all carry `kind:linux` gives no way to tell whether labels, capabilities, or the claim race was the real cause.

## W2-4 — observation: the documented grace is not the deadline — the real deadline is the next 30 s sweep, so an agent returning up to 25.1 s past `UNIFIED_QUEUED_RUN_GRACE` still saved its run in 6 of 7 trials
- **Invariant:** none violated. This is the boundary measurement the scenario exists to produce, and it is the number an operator planning a maintenance window actually needs.
- **Severity:** minor (observation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-4-queued-reaper.md`, Part B, seven trials driven by `w2-4/partB-trial.sh` and `w2-4/partB-phase.sh`. Raw evidence: `w2-4/partB-{m5a,m1a,p1a,p15a,p25a,ph5a,ph1a}-run.txt` plus the matching `-poll.txt` 0.2 s samplers and `-trigger.json`; sweep instants from `w2-4/partB-sweeps.txt`; `w2-4/partB-calibration.txt`, `w2-4/partB-calibration2.txt`.
- **Observed:** Each trial triggered `edge-tick` with both agents stopped, then returned `agent1` at a chosen offset from `created_at + 30 s`. **Outcomes are keyed to the MEASURED registration offset, not the intended one** (the `agents`-row appearance from the 0.2 s sampler, so ≤0.2 s late as an estimate of registration; the statement log gives the exact value only for the backlog trial):

  | trial | intended | `created_at` | boundary `+30 s` | `agents` row seen | measured offset | first sweep past boundary | outcome |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | m5a | -5 s | 22:26:18.611925 | 22:26:48.612 | 22:26:43.784779 | **-4.827 s** | 22:26:59.016 | agent won -> `Succeeded` |
  | m1a | -1 s | 22:27:50.008246 | 22:28:20.008 | 22:28:19.123696 | **-0.885 s** | 22:28:29.051 | agent won -> `Succeeded` |
  | p1a | +1 s | 22:29:21.310260 | 22:29:51.310 | 22:29:52.408356 | **+1.098 s** | 22:29:59.102 | agent won -> `Succeeded` |
  | p15a | +15 s | 22:33:32.323584 | 22:34:02.324 | 22:34:17.399873 | **+15.076 s** | 22:34:29.221 | agent won -> `Succeeded` |
  | p25a | +25 s | 22:35:03.617224 | 22:35:33.617 | 22:35:58.733482 | **+25.116 s** | 22:35:59.261 | agent won by **0.528 s** -> `Succeeded` |
  | ph5a | +5 s, phase-aligned | 22:37:57.283194 | 22:38:27.283 | 22:38:32.369265 | **+5.086 s** | 22:38:29.333 | **reaper won** -> `Failed 22:38:29.336885` |
  | ph1a | +1 s, phase-aligned | 22:39:57.265501 | 22:40:27.266 | 22:40:28.375794 | **+1.110 s** | 22:40:29.376 | agent won by **1.000 s** -> `Succeeded` |

  **Each of the three plan-specified naive offsets (-5, -1, +1) ran once**, plus +15 and +25 once each and one phase-locked repeat at +5 and +1 (seven trials total, `w2-4/partB-{m5a,m1a,p1a,p15a,p25a,ph5a,ph1a}-run.txt`). Further repeats on the naive axis were judged worthless once the statement log showed *why* they all survived — no sweep fell in the window regardless of the intended offset — so the remaining budget went to phase-locking and to Part B5 instead. n=1 per naive offset is therefore a deliberate allocation decision, not an oversight.

  **The offset from the grace boundary does not predict the outcome; the distance to the next sweep does.** In every trial the winner is whichever of "registration" and "first sweep past `created_at + 30 s`" came first. The three offsets the plan asked for (-5, -1, +1) all survived, and so did +15 and +25 — not because 25 s of slack is designed in, but because those triggers happened to land where the boundary fell 25-27 s before the next sweep. The two `ph*` trials were therefore re-run with the trigger phase-locked so the boundary fell ~2.4 s before a sweep (`partB-phase.sh`, using the measured grid), and at that phase **+5.086 s lost while +1.110 s won**: the reaper failed `56e1f05f-...` at `22:38:29.336885`, **2.054 s past the boundary**, and `agent1` registered **3.032 s after the run was already dead**. So the survivable post-grace window is **uniform on [0, 30) s by construction, not by sampling** — it follows from the sweep grid being evenly spaced and the mutex/agent-return instant being unsynchronized to it, not from the seven trials themselves, which are far too few to establish a sampled distribution; the trials confirm the mechanism at two points, they do not measure the shape.
  - **Reap latencies past eligibility, all under the nominal 30 s sweep interval:** **8.147 s** (Part A, `edge-tick` `dbed9313`, `created_at 19:42:17.472718` -> `updated_at 19:42:55.619999`), **2.054 s** (ph5a), and **~20.5 s** (Part A's `edge-podcap-job` run, whose eligibility began when the last `agents` row was deleted between `19:42:04.771` and `19:42:05.069`, failed at `19:42:25.60296` — so 20.53-20.83 s, the spread being the sampling gap around the deletion). **Budgeted against the nominal 30 s interval per the corrected rule; `interval / N` is a query-load figure only.**
  - **The sweep grid, measured directly:** every execution of `ListUnclaimableQueuedRuns`' SELECT over `22:26:59.016` -> `22:45:29.510` (the last captured execution, not `22:45:33`) is **98 executions in 38 clusters = 2.58 per nominal 30 s interval** (`w2-4/partB-sweeps.txt`), clusters landing on wall seconds `:29.0x` and `:59.0x-.27`, between-cluster gap 30.00-30.02 s, nothing in between. **This independently reproduces W2-3's clustering result** (`FINDINGS.md:733`) on a second query and a second scenario: the count is ~2-3 per interval, the arrivals are simultaneous, and worst-case latency is bounded by 30 s, not by `30 / N`.
  - Two incidental measurements from the same captures: Pending->Queued took **109.9 ms** (`edge-tick`, Part A) and **21.8 ms** (`edge-podcap-job`, Part D) with **no agent** and **two agents** respectively — queueing consults no agent, exactly as the plan says. And `docker start` -> `agents`-row insert is **0.400 s** via raw `docker start` (`w2-4/partB-calibration2.txt`) but **1.697 s** via `docker compose start`, because compose re-resolves the `agent-enroll` dependency first (`w2-4/partB-calibration.txt`); the trials use the raw form and subtract 0.400 s as lead.
- **Expected:** `docs/high-availability.md:352-356` says queued runs "are failed once they have waited longer than the queued-run reaper grace ... Raise it if such outages can exceed the default." That is true as a *lower* bound and silent about the upper one. The actual failure time is `created_at + grace + U(0, interval)`, so with the shipped defaults a run is failed somewhere in **[5 m, 5 m 30 s]** after creation, and an operator sizing a maintenance window against "5m" is reasoning about the wrong end of the interval.
- **Notes:** `UNIFIED_QUEUED_RUN_GRACE` is the only knob and it controls the floor; documenting the `+ up to one 30 s sweep` term would cost one sentence. The phase-locking technique (`partB-phase.sh`) is reusable: because the per-tick jobs' sweeps sit on a stable grid at `(epoch mod interval) ~= const` for as long as the replicas are not restarted, any later wave that needs a reaper race to be a coin flip rather than a 3 % long shot can *place* its trigger instead of repeating it.

## W2-4 — observation: a capability-unschedulable run is left `Queued` forever exactly as the code comment intends, but the exemption is conditional on an unusable agent staying connected, and nothing run-scoped ever says why
- **Invariant:** none violated — this is the documented-intent check the plan asked for, and the intent holds.
- **Severity:** minor (observation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-4-queued-reaper.md`, Part D. Raw evidence: `w2-4/armD-jobapply.txt`, `w2-4/armD-trigger.json`, `w2-4/armD-poll.txt`, `w2-4/armD-surfaces.txt`, `w2-4/armA-poll.txt`, `w2-4/armA-logs.txt`, `w2-4/baseline-gate.txt`.
- **Observed:** `edge-podcap-job` (a `podTemplate` with a pod-level `nodeSelector`, so `dsl.RequiredCaps` infers `pod`) with `agentSelector: [kind:linux]`, against two live agents advertising `["native","container"]`. `GET /api/v1/jobs/edge-podcap-job/schedulability` returned `{"requiredCaps":["pod"],"selector":["kind:linux"],"satisfiable":false,"reason":"no registered agent provides capability [pod]"}`. Run `f9ae8786-...` reached `Queued` **21.8 ms** after creation and stayed `Queued` across **384.4 s** of continuous 5 s sampling with two live `agents` rows throughout — **12.8x the 30 s grace in force and 1.28x the 5 m shipped default** — with zero `queued-run reaper` log lines naming it. Confirms `internal/store/postgres.go:1264-1274` and `queuedrun_reaper.go:18-28`.
  - **What surfaces it (each checked, `w2-4/armD-surfaces.txt`):** `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}` -> `{"status":"Queued"}` and nothing else; `api.Run` carries no `requiredCaps` field even though the column is populated (`SELECT required_caps` -> `{pod}`). `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}/logs` -> `[]`. Controller logs -> the only two lines naming the run id are my own HTTP access records. `/metrics` -> `unifiedcd_runs_current{status="Queued"} 1` and `unifiedcd_agents{state="alive"} 2`, i.e. a count with no age and no reason (`internal/metrics/collector.go:56-59` exports nothing else). **The single surface that names the cause is `GET /api/v1/jobs/{name}/schedulability`, and it is job-scoped**: an operator holding a run id must first map it to a job and then know the endpoint exists. It is also evaluated against the job's *current* spec, not the run's `runs.spec` snapshot.
  - **The exemption is conditional, and that half is not in the comment.** The comment says such a run is "intentionally left Queued rather than auto-failed by this reaper". That holds only while a label-matching live agent exists, because the `NOT EXISTS` conjunct does not care *why* nothing can claim the run. When both agents were cleanly stopped for Part A, this same run — `Queued` for 458.0 s by then and never claimable by anything on the rig — was failed **~20.5 s later** at `19:42:25.60296`, with the label-blaming reason line quoted in the entry above. The "left Queued on purpose" state therefore ends the moment the *unusable* agent disconnects.
- **Expected:** `docs/troubleshooting.md:74-130` documents the banner and the API and matches what was observed. But `docs/troubleshooting.md:32-73` — `Run stays Queued forever`, whose documented cause is "**no connected agent satisfies the job's `agentSelector`**, or every agent that does is already at its concurrency limit", and whose documented remedy is "Cancel a run stuck this way with `unified-cli run cancel <run-id>`" — describes precisely the queued-run reaper's *target* case and never mentions that the run is failed automatically after `UNIFIED_QUEUED_RUN_GRACE`. Part A refutes that section's own premise: a label-unclaimable run does **not** stay `Queued` forever, it is `Failed` in 38.1 s under a 30 s grace. The section predates the reaper and is stale.
- **Notes:** Two cheap improvements, both already computed somewhere in the codebase: expose `requiredCaps` on `api.Run` (or add a run-scoped schedulability read) so a run id alone reaches the diagnosis; and append the same explanatory line the reaper writes on the paths it *does* act on at the moment an unschedulable run is created, rather than only in a WebUI banner on another page. Also correct `docs/troubleshooting.md:32-73` to say the run will be auto-failed and to point at the reaper's own reason line in the run log.

## W2-4 — observation: the queued-run grace is measured from `created_at`, so a run that spent it legitimately blocked on a mutex enters `Queued` already reapable and gets **zero** grace — the 29.8 s it survived came entirely from the sweep phase, not from the grace
- **Invariant:** none violated on the I1 limb (nothing was executing, no agent was alive). I5 (bounded recovery) is cited for the wording mismatch below, and the citation is weak: recovery was bounded, the *documented* bound is just not the one the code applies.
- **Severity:** minor (observation, with the measured window)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-4-queued-reaper.md`, Part C, driven by `w2-4/partC.sh`. Raw evidence: `w2-4/armC-run.txt`, `w2-4/armC-poll.txt` (0.2 s sampler across the whole chain), `w2-4/armC-ids.txt`. **No DB mutation anywhere in this arm** — every transition is the product's own, including the mutex release.
- **Observed:** The chain, all times on the DB clock, from `w2-4/armC-run.txt` and `w2-4/armC-poll.txt`:
  - `edge-mutex-hog` `ed048344-...` created `22:54:57.284107`, claimed by `agent1` at `22:54:57.402385`, holding `edge-mutex` from `22:54:57.324655`.
  - `edge-sideeffect` `bd557f17-...` (same mutex) created `22:55:06.437476` and stayed **`Pending`** — `tryQueueRun` rolls back on the `mutex_holders` unique violation (`internal/store/postgres.go:546-555`) and leaves the status untouched, exactly as the W2 plan's corrected W2-9 note says.
  - Both agents `kill-hard`ed at `22:57:14.319` (host clock), so the `agents` rows survived with frozen `last_seen_at` (`agent1 22:57:05.430145`, `agent2 22:57:05.65128`). At that moment the victim had been `Pending` for **125.9 s**.
  - The **stuck-run** reaper failed the hog at `22:58:59.806587` — `last_seen_at + 90 s = 22:58:35.430`, so **24.376 s past eligibility**, again inside the nominal 30 s interval — and `FinishRun`'s transaction released `edge-mutex` with it (`mutex_holders` 1 → 0 in the same sample).
  - **196.9 ms later** the scheduler transitioned the victim `Pending → Queued`: `updated_at = 22:59:00.003445`. Its `created_at` was already **233.566 s** old, i.e. **7.8× the 30 s grace**, so it was reapable the instant it became `Queued`.
  - The **queued-run** reaper failed it at `22:59:29.818383` (reason line at `22:59:29.815423`), releasing the mutex the victim had itself just acquired.
  - **`queued_window` = `22:59:00.003445` → `22:59:29.818383` = 29.815 s.** Total age at failure **263.381 s**, of which **233.566 s Pending** and **29.815 s Queued**.
- **Expected:** `docs/high-availability.md:352-356` says queued runs "are failed once they have **waited** longer than the queued-run reaper grace", and `queuedRunGraceDefault`'s own comment (`cmd/controller/main.go:98-101`) reads "how long a run may sit **Queued** with no eligible live agent before being failed". Both describe a Queued-time budget. `ListUnclaimableQueuedRuns` implements `r.created_at < NOW() - minAge` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1279`) and there is no `queued_at` column to implement the other reading with (`internal/store/migrations/001_init.up.sql:213-226` — only `created_at` and `updated_at`). The doc statement is a published contract; the `main.go` comment is an unexported function's own comment and is cited only as evidence of intent.
- **Notes:**
  - **Severity reasoning, stated explicitly because the plan's test does not discriminate here.** The plan's rule is "major if the run is failed before one full claim poll could have occurred, else observation with the measured window". The claim long-poll on this rig is 30 s (`ClaimDetached(..., "30s", ...)`, `internal/agent/agent.go:411-415`) and the measured Queued window was **29.815 s** — just inside it, so the rule points at *major* by 0.185 s. But the rule cannot actually discriminate in this arm: **no agent was alive to poll at all**, so "one full claim poll could have occurred" is false for a second, unrelated reason. Filed as an observation on that ground, not by rounding the window.
  - **What is transferable is the clock, and the number that shows it is 0, not 29.8.** The grace contributed **zero** seconds of protection after the Queued transition — the age gate was already satisfied by 203.6 s. The 29.815 s the run survived came entirely from where the transition landed on the sweep grid, which is uniform on [0, 30) s (see the Part B observation above). A victim whose mutex is released 1 s *before* a sweep gets ~1 s of Queued life, not 29.8 s. **The 29.815 s is one draw from that distribution, not a bound.**
  - **The reap instant is consistent with the same sweep grid, but this is derived, not captured.** Statement logging was already reverted when Part C ran (`w2-4/partB-stmtlog-off.txt`, `22:46:50`), so there is no direct sweep capture for it. Extrapolating Part B's grid with its own measured period — `22:26:59.016` → `22:31:59.148` is 10 ticks in 300.132 s, i.e. **30.013 s/tick**, so the grid drifts ~13 ms per tick rather than sitting still — lands the `22:59:29` cluster at ≈`22:59:29.8`, matching the observed `22:59:29.815` reason-line write and the hog's `22:58:59.806` stuck-run reap. **Consistent, not measured.**
  - **Where this stops being cosmetic is in combination with the major above.** A run that has been `Pending` a long time (mutex, semaphore, or a `git://` `uses:` awaiting resolution — `postgres.go:513-518` skips those the same way) becomes `Queued` the moment its blocker clears. If the blocker clears because a full agent outage is *ending*, that run is simultaneously (a) instantly past `minAge` with no grace left and (b) inside the stale-list window of the sweep that is running as the agents re-register. Both halves were observed in this scenario, in different arms.
  - **Not established:** the git-resolution variant of the same setup. `tryQueueRun` skips unresolved `git://` `uses:` steps before touching status, so such a run also sits `Pending` accumulating age, and this campaign has no git fixture (deferred with W1-7). Same mechanism, untested.

## W2-5 — a `call:` child whose one link report is lost becomes permanently unreachable: the parent's cancel cascade walked an empty child list and the orphan ran to `Succeeded` 38.840 s later, writing 37 further side effects after the parent was terminal
- **Invariant:** **I6** (zombie containment — *measure, don't judge*, per the design spec's own caveat at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:53`, so the 38.840 s lifetime and 37-marker count below are the deliverable of this limb and not its verdict) and **I7** (state display consistency: `GET /api/v1/runs/a833b977-...` carries no `calledBy`, the parent's run detail carries no call step at all, and both contradict the reality that this child was created by that parent). **I1 is cited as the closest fit only** — the orphan did reach exactly one terminal state (`Succeeded`), so I1's literal text is *not* what is contradicted; what I1 is being stretched to cover is that the run reached that state with no accounting edge to the parent that spawned it. Same "closest fit only" shape as the precedent at `FINDINGS.md:489`. **The violation itself rests on a published documented contract — `docs/jobs.md:671-673` — not on an invariant.** **NOT I3:** campaign I3 is *no lock leaks* (design spec `:50`; this file uses it correctly at `FINDINGS.md:350`), and no mutex, semaphore or named-lock slot is involved in either workload here — an earlier draft of this entry relabelled I3 as "no orphaned side effects", which is not a campaign invariant at all.
- **Severity:** major
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w2-5-unlinked-child.md`, Part B (Part A is the control; see the per-control injection-state bullet below — **control 1 is the uninjected one**). Raw evidence: `w2-5/baseline-gate.txt`, `w2-5/partB-inject.txt`, `w2-5/partB-rearm.txt`, `w2-5/partB-inject2.txt`, `w2-5/partB-b4-unlinked.txt`, `w2-5/partB-b4b-b7-cancel.txt`, `w2-5/partB-b8-childpoll.txt`, `w2-5/partB-b9-final.txt`, `w2-5/partB-b5-b9-logs.txt`, `w2-5/agent2-full.log`, `w2-5/nginx-full.log`, `w2-5/armA-link.txt`, `w2-5/armA-cascade.txt`, `w2-5/armA-childlog.txt`, `w2-5/armA2-control2.txt`, `w2-5/armA2-childlog-raw.txt`.
- **Observed:** `edge-call-parent` run `b556c457-1d78-487a-94ce-8d896260aad6` (claimed by `agent2`) created child run `a833b977-84df-49e6-b70e-6aeacbb7b19e` (`edge-call-child`, claimed by `agent1`) while `agent2`'s step-report endpoint was refused at nginx and its child-create endpoint was not. Timeline, DB clock except where noted:

  ```
  23:43:55.878014  parent created            (runs.created_at)
  23:43:56.471154  parent claimed by agent2
  23:43:56.479124  step_reports row: step 0 'prelude' Running
  23:44:08.510227  steplock armed for agent2 (probe confirmed 403 at 23:44:09.321, host clock)
  23:44:16.584001  agent2: ERROR "permanent error, giving up retry" status=403   <-- prelude terminal report
  23:44:16.589078  CHILD RUN CREATED  a833b977...  (POST .../children, not blocked)
  23:44:16.590933  agent2: INFO "call: child run created" childRunId=a833b977...  (agent clock)
  23:44:16          nginx: 3 x POST /api/v1/agents/agent2/steps -> 403           <-- the link report is one of these
  23:44:17.508838  child claimed by agent1; first /data/child.log marker 23:44:17
  23:44:49.777573  steplock cleared (nothing after the child's creation is injected)
  23:45:09.110168  PARENT CANCELLED via POST /api/v1/runs/{id}/cancel -> 204     (api_runs.go:382 cascade)
  23:45:11.483120  agent2: "received cancellation signal from master"  (+2.373 s)
  23:45:11          nginx: POST .../agents/agent2/steps -> 200 87                <-- the "self-heal" report
  23:45:11          nginx: POST .../runs/b556c457.../finish -> 200 94            <-- alreadyFinalized
  23:45:47.950560  CHILD SUCCEEDED   (orphan lifetime 38.840 s past the parent)
  ```

  **The unlinked state, measured with `ListChildRunIDs`' own query.** At `23:44:47` and again after the run finished, `SELECT child_run_id FROM step_reports WHERE run_id='b556c457-...' AND child_run_id IS NOT NULL` returned **0 rows** (`w2-5/partB-b4-unlinked.txt`, `w2-5/partB-b9-final.txt`). The parent's *entire* step history is one row — `step 0 'prelude' status='Running'` with a NULL `ended_at`. **There is no row for step 1 at all**, so not even the call step's name survives. The reverse query is equally empty: `SELECT count(*) FROM step_reports sr JOIN runs r ON r.id=sr.run_id WHERE sr.child_run_id='a833b977-...'` (`GetRunParent`'s predicate) returned **0**, and `GET /api/v1/runs/a833b977-...` carried **no `calledBy` object** while the control's did.

  **The consequence, on the side effect.** `/data/child.log` holds markers 1-90 at `23:44:17` … `23:45:46` (`w2-5/partB-b9-final.txt`): the child ran **all 90 iterations to completion**, of which **37 markers (`child,54` … `child,90`, `23:45:10` … `23:45:46`) are strictly after the parent's terminal timestamp** `23:45:09.110168`, the last one **≈36.9 s** after it (`23:45:46` − `23:45:09.110168` = 36.890 s; **the markers are second-granular, so this figure carries no more than ±1 s and is deliberately not quoted to three decimals** — only the `Succeeded` headline of 38.840 s is DB-clock-to-DB-clock and therefore exact). Cross-clock comparison is safe at this granularity: the child's DB `created_at` `23:44:16.589078` and `agent2`'s own log line for the same event `23:44:16.590933` are **1.855 ms** apart in the causally required order, bounding agent-to-DB skew well under the second granularity of the markers.
